### Formal semantics (Lean 4)

The definition of the behavior language: the engine input, configurations, the expression language and the step relation, written in Lean 4 as an executable reference that the Rust engine is checked against, with its basic properties proved.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Evaluate a run to a trace in the documented format
- Prove determinism of the step function, at most one fired transition per behavior per step, and termination under the step limit
- Serve as the reference for differential checking of the Rust engine on goldens and generated inputs

#### Relationships in

- Depends on → Simulation engine: The engine is checked against the formal semantics, which defines what it must compute (planned, WG-4685).
- Depends on → Proof export: Proofs are stated against the formal semantics.
- Depends on → Exhaustive explorer: The successor relation takes the semantics' deterministic step after each choice; the explorer runs the semantics' own step function.
- Depends on → Resource model: Resources, grants, bounds and their invariants are defined in Lean first (planned, WG-4664).
- Depends on → Workload model: Arrivals, links, latency, timers and the SplitMix64 streams are defined in Lean first (planned, WG-4715).
- Depends on → Performance metrics: Metrics are defined in Lean as a function of the run (planned, WG-4715).

#### Known unknowns

- Format 2 is defined in formal/Exegra/Format2.lean for the constructs built so far (instances, routes, resources and resource faults, windows, workloads, links, timers, request lifecycle and measured outputs); formal/Exegra/Resource.lean proves the held-units and queue-bound invariants of a settled resource, the strict FIFO queue of a collapse included. Not defined yet: crash-instance with incarnations, meters and cost (planned: WG-4741).
- lift_agrees is stated in formal/Exegra/Lift.lean with its proof left as sorry (pinned on sorryAx); it is checked differentially by tools/differential.js --lift on every liftable golden and generated input (planned: WG-4664).
- The determinism property over seeds is not stated as a theorem for format 2 yet (planned: WG-4741).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `formal/`, `tools/gen-engine-inputs.js`, `tools/differential.js`
- Notes: A Lake project (Lean 4, toolchain pinned in formal/lean-toolchain, standard library only): Exegra/Value, Json, Number, Expr, Input, Trace and Semantics define the semantics, Exegra/Properties proves its properties, Main is the exegra-lean executable (engine input JSON on stdin, trace JSON on stdout). npm run build:formal runs lake build in formal/; formal/.lake/ is build output, ignored by git and outside the traceability surface. tools/differential.js compares the native Rust engine with exegra-lean on the goldens and on seeded inputs from tools/gen-engine-inputs.js.

### Proof export

Exports a scenario's engine input and its state-invariant and temporal (never, precedes, eventually) claims to a Lean file whose theorems state the verified explorer's result per claim, checks the file with Lean and reports each claim as verified (for all N configurations within the bound, or on every complete run), a counterexample run, or incomplete, with the axioms the proof uses. Proving invariants for every reachable configuration at every depth, by induction, is planned (WG-4686).

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Build the scenario's engine input exactly as simulate does and collect the state-invariant and temporal claims that list the scenario and apply to it; a claim that names behaviors or an expression outside the scenario is reported not evaluable
- Write formal/generated/<scenario>.lean: the engine input text, its decoded Lean term checked against it (input_decodes), and per claim a theorem explore env (claimInv expr) bound maxStates = result, discharged by native_decide
- Check the file with Lake, loading the modules' compiled code, and report per claim the method lean-exhaustive, the bound, N, the theorem, the file, the axioms and what is trusted
- Render a counterexample as simulate shows a run: the step list with the delivery choices, the active faults, and the request-log lines of contract events
- For a temporal claim write the theorem tverdict form env bound maxStates = result (from the exegra-lean temporal protocol), whose meaning is tverdict_sound and tverdict_real, and render a violating run with the reason in the trace check's words

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Proofs are stated against the formal semantics.
- Depends on → Exhaustive explorer: Counterexamples to induction come from bounded exploration.
- Calls → Simulation contract: Builds the scenario's engine input with simulationInput, exactly as simulate does.
- Calls → Expression language: Parses claim expressions and evaluates them on a counterexample's last configuration to explain it.
- Calls → Contract conformance: Renders a counterexample's contract events as request-log lines.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Claim evaluator: Claims proved in Lean are reported as verified by proof (planned, WG-4686).
- Calls → Agent command line: The prove command explores a scenario and checks its state invariants in Lean.

#### Known unknowns

- Results are established by native_decide, so each theorem depends on an auxiliary axiom that trusts the Lean compiler and runtime. Kernel reduction (decide) is not feasible: the expression language's number literals go through Float.scaleB and the visited set hashes strings with String.hash, both opaque to the kernel.
- The unbounded proof of an invariant (by induction over the step relation, with the explorer supplying counterexamples to induction) is not built yet (planned: WG-4686).
- Claim results are reported by exegra prove only; evaluate and verify do not show them yet.
- Negatives of a claim are not explored in Lean: prove checks that a claim holds on every configuration of its scenarios, not that its negatives violate it (WG-4711).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/proof-export.js`, `tools/prove.js`, `formal/Exegra/Prove.lean`
- Notes: src/core/proof-export.js (pure) builds the exploration request, the Lean proof file and the per-claim results; tools/prove.js runs lake build, the exegra-lean explore protocol and lake env lean on the generated file (formal/generated/, ignored by git), with the modules' shared libraries (precompileModules) loaded; formal/Exegra/Prove.lean holds claimInv, decodesTo, the Lean term printer of an input and the explore protocol of exegra-lean. The command is exegra prove (cli/commands.js).

### Contract conformance

Lets an event be a request, a response or an error of a contract at a boundary, so that a simulated trace is checked and reads like a request log. Holds the type-string parser shared by the static and the trace checks.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Parse the type strings of payload fields and contract inputs and outputs (string, number, boolean, null, literals, unions, arrays); a type it cannot read is opaque and every value fits it
- Derive from a contract the fields and error codes it allows for a request, a response and an error
- Check a trace: for each step whose event is a contract event, the payload values fit the declared types, a request carries every input that is not optional, an error code is among the contract's errors, and a response or error answers an earlier unanswered request of the same contract (matched by the correlation field when the event declares one, otherwise by count); each violation is a failed automatic check carrying the step
- Render a trace as request-log lines (src/core/contract-trace.js, requestLog): the contract's binding (method and path, or operation) or else the event name for a request, 'ok' for a response and 'refused <code>' for an error; steps that are not contract events give no line
- Read traces and models only: the engine, its Lean semantics and the engine input format are untouched

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Validator: Checks the payload of every contract event against the fields, types and errors of its contract.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: checkTrace adds the automatic checks of the contract events of a trace.
- Calls → Application session: Derives the request log of the loaded run for the simulator panel.
- Calls → Agent command line: simulate lists the request log of the run.
- Calls → Test suites: Core tests exercise the static and trace checks and the request log.
- Calls → Proof export: Renders a counterexample's contract events as request-log lines.

#### Known unknowns

- Object and generic types (for example {text, hash} or Operation[]) are opaque to the type check: their values are accepted unchecked.
- A response is paired with its request by one correlation field or by count; there is no timeout or ordering rule for unanswered requests at the end of a run.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/type-expr.js`, `src/core/contract-trace.js`

### Data model

Makes records, their fields, owning component, personal-data class, keys and the copies of them part of the specification, so that erasure, identity keying, shared-copy and freshness rules are computed over the model instead of read from hand-maintained tables.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Define the record kind (owner, key, fields with type, personal class, refersTo a record or the principal, role, retention) and the copy kind (of, fields, heldBy, scope, freshness, erasure, dependsOnAuthority), validated by the generic parser with positions
- Check that a record's key and a copy's fields name declared fields, and that a numeric freshness or erasure bound has a unit
- Compute the five data rules per copy, record and field: an erasure point on every copy holding a personal field or a principal reference; no shared copy holding a principal reference, a personal field or authority-dependent data; party fields that refer to the principal by a non-name type; a freshness bound on every copy; a relationship path between a copy's holder and the owner of each record it copies
- Report every finding as a validation warning naming the entity and the field, and evaluate the data-model claim method from the same findings
- Answer the copies of a record and the fields a copy holds for the inspector and the Data projection

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Model queries: Builds the component graph to find a path between a copy's holder and a record's owner.
- Reads → Meta-model: The record and copy kinds and their enums are declared in the meta-model.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Validator: Runs the data rules and the record and copy consistency checks as part of validation.
- Calls → Claim evaluator: Evaluates data-model claims from the findings of the data rules.
- Calls → Projection system: The Data projection draws records, copies, owners and holders, and marks copies with findings.
- Calls → Semantic inspector: Lists a record's fields and copies and a copy's source fields.

#### Known unknowns

- A field counts as a name when its type text contains the word name or handle; there is no type system for field types, so a party field typed as an opaque string passes rule party-by-id (WG-4697).
- Retention is free text: nothing checks that a record's retention and its copies' erasure points agree, and a copy of a copy cannot be declared, since of names records only (WG-4697).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/data-model.js`
- Notes: The record and copy kinds are built-in meta-model kinds; the rules are hand-written in data-model.js and run from validate.js and claims.js.

### Resource model

Resources with capacity, request classes, queue disciplines and bounds, and the service demand of transitions: the part of format 2 that makes a pool, a connection limit or a CPU a queueing station.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Hold per resource its capacity, discipline (fifo or priority), request classes with priority, reserved units (at most the class limit), limit, queue bound and maximum wait, the overflow rule (refuse, drop, shed-oldest) and an optional load-dependent demand factor: a step table (the last row holds beyond it) or exact USL parameters evaluated at each grant
- Record the use, acquire and release effects in phase 2 and apply them when the step settles: release, enqueue, grant by discipline and eligibility, then class bounds in declaration order and the resource bound; holdings are tracked per instance, resource and class, and release names its class
- Draw service demand at the grant from the stream demand:<behavior>:<transition>:<effect> (effect index from 0), against the faults active at that time, and scale it once, rounding up, by the demand factor of the occupancy at the grant and by the combined scale-demand factor reduced to lowest terms (a part of 2^32 or more, or a service time of 2^53 or more, is a failed step); the service time is fixed at the grant
- Delete a request's wait-timeout entry on every path that removes it (grant, bound refusal or drop, shed, crash), so a wait-timeout step always finds its request
- Refuse statically a request whose units can never be granted, also after the fault mask is applied
- Apply scale-demand, set-capacity, set-class-limit, collapse-classes and replace-demand, untimed or within windows through fault-edge steps, the value in effect being a function of the active set (last in the input's faults order, product for scale-demand, any for collapse), without preempting held units; return waiting requests to their own classes when a collapse ends
- Apply crash-instance: an incarnation per instance, recorded on targeted, routed and from fields and on the instance's service-done and wait-timeout entries; at the crash remove timers, waiting requests with their timeouts and in-service requests with their service-done entries, release holdings, draw the restart time, broadcast notify; drop stale entries per behavior; restart edges restore only their own incarnation; a crash of a missing or down instance does nothing
- Keep held units within class limits and capacity, and waiting requests within their bounds, as invariants proved of the step; deadlock freedom is not guaranteed and is left to claims and the explorer

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Resources, grants, bounds and their invariants are defined in Lean first (planned, WG-4664).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Simulation engine: Settles resources after each step and runs service-done and wait-timeout entries (planned, WG-4664, WG-4715).
- Calls → Simulation contract: Lowers resources, classes and service demand into the version-2 engine input (planned).
- Depends on → Load aspect: Saturation and backpressure are those of the resources of format 2.

#### Contracts bound

- Engine input, format version 2 (`engine-input-v2`): provider, via `simulation-contract-calls-resource-model`

#### Known unknowns

- Built (WG-4664, WG-4729) in the Lean semantics and the Rust engine, resource faults included (set-capacity, set-class-limit, collapse-classes, scale-demand, replace-demand; untimed, or windowed through fault-edge steps), and derived from a specification's resources by simulationInput. crash-instance with incarnations is refused by both engines, and the grantable-units check is not repeated after the untimed fault mask (planned: WG-4741).
- Processor sharing is not part of format 2; CPU is a FCFS multi-server station (planned: WG-4719).
- Credit-based backpressure between components is not represented (planned: WG-4722); autoscaling and cold start are not represented (planned: WG-4720).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `formal/Exegra/Resource.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Random.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Format2.lean`, `formal/Exegra/Lift.lean`, `engine/exegra-engine/src/v2.rs`, `engine/exegra-engine/src/random.rs`
- Notes: WG-4664 part of format 2: defined in Lean (Resource, Random, Format2, Lift), ported to Rust (v2.rs, random.rs), and checked differentially on generated version-2 inputs (tools/gen-engine-inputs.js --v2, tools/differential.js). Its claims stay not evaluated until recorded evidence supports them.

### Workload model

Workloads that drive a scenario from outside its behaviors: open arrival processes (constant, Poisson, ramp, step, on/off, attack floods) with client and address identities and payload generators, closed populations with think time, populations of client behaviors, links with bandwidth, latency, ordering and fan-out, and timers.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Generate open arrivals as instants of b entries, one future instant per workload in the queue, computed from the scheduled time of the previous instant when its original first entry is taken (a delayed copy is never first, and a per-workload count of created instants creates each instant once); draw, in this order, the instant time (Poisson, spent draws included), the batch size, then per entry the client and the payload fields; stop at the horizon
- Validate phases (from < until, sorted, non-overlapping, repeat at least the last until; rates count instants) and schedule constant phases exactly, with j reset per phase and repetition, and Poisson phases (constant or ramp) by inverting the integrated rate against a unit exponential sample, with exact integer square roots and rounding to the nearest base unit: one draw per instant, no thinning and no rejection loop; refuse a phase whose mean interarrival is below 100 base units
- Attach the request id, the client and the address; group users onto addresses by a drawn group-size distribution (sizes below 1 count as 1)
- Run closed populations (issue with a workload-wide request counter, wait for the matching reply, think; a timeout ends the request with outcome timeout and a late reply is ignored) and population workloads whose clients are keyed instances of a behavior created at the start of the run
- Send emits over links: FIFO transfer with time ceil(size * perSecond / bandwidth), then at link-done the latency draw, the delivery entry and the next transfer; ordered links deliver in send order; fan-out keeps one bounded queue per receiver at the link's full bandwidth and drops its oldest waiting message, never the one in transfer
- Set, replace and cancel named timers by their stable id, so that a delayed timer is still cancelled
- Lower specification-level rates, durations and distributions to integers and tables on unbounded BigInt: the exponential and normal quantile tables defined by integer algorithms (exp and ln in 96-bit fixed point, a 192-bit fixed-point normal CDF with bisection), equal to the correctly rounded values and pinned by the SHA-256 a23cd2a9566c106701375d5cf75ef982b8db6f3e52c199c47e6239649c840950 of their canonical text; log-normal sigma at most 8; a lowered value of 2^53 base units or more is a lowering error

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Arrivals, links, latency, timers and the SplitMix64 streams are defined in Lean first (planned, WG-4715).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Simulation engine: Queues the next arrival of a workload when one is taken, runs link transfers and timers (planned, WG-4715).
- Calls → Simulation contract: Lowers workloads, rates, durations, distributions and links into the version-2 engine input (planned).
- Depends on → Load aspect: The load aspect drives scenarios with the workloads of format 2 (planned, WG-4668 on WG-4715).

#### Contracts bound

- Engine input, format version 2 (`engine-input-v2`): provider, via `simulation-contract-calls-workload-model`

#### Known unknowns

- Built (WG-4715, WG-4729): open, closed and population workloads, address groups, links (bandwidth, ordering, queue bounds, fan-out, latency) and timers in both engines, derived from a specification's workloads and links by simulationInput (src/core/sim-input.js).
- Packet-level networking and packet-rate floods are not represented: a link is a FIFO byte pipe with a latency distribution, one step per message (aggregated mode planned: WG-4718).
- Matchmaking gather is writable only as a hand-written behavior (primitive planned: WG-4721).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/distributions.js`, `formal/Exegra/Workload.lean`, `engine/exegra-engine/src/workload.rs`
- Notes: WG-4715, in progress. Built: the integer lowering of distributions (src/core/distributions.js; every reference output of docs/simulation.md and the pinned SHA-256 reproduced, tests/core/distributions.test.js); open workloads (constant and Poisson by inversion of the integrated rate with exact integer square roots, phases, ramps, repetition, batches, clients picked uniformly or round-robin, payload literals, samples, weighted choices and counters, maxArrivals, the first-entry rule with a per-workload count of created instants) defined in formal/Exegra/Workload.lean and Format2.lean, ported to the Rust engine (workload.rs, v2.rs) and checked differentially on generated workload inputs (tools/gen-engine-inputs.js, tools/differential.js). Proved: an instant is always before the horizon (nextTime_lt_horizon) and a workload never creates more arrival entries than maxArrivals (makeInstant_arrivals_le).

### Performance metrics

The measured outputs of a run as a pure function of its steps: throughput, outcomes, utilisation, queue length, waiting time, response-time percentiles, false positives, transition counts, link use and cost, per resource, class, link and boundary, over a declared window and in time series, all as integers; and meters, windowed measurements that behaviors read.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Follow the request lifecycle of docs/simulation.md: a request starts when its start entry is first taken as head, ends as error at once when that entry is dropped, crashed, stale or handled by no transition, ends at the first delivered end event with its key (delivered: head of a deliver step that does not fail), ends as timeout on a closed-client timeout, and is otherwise censored; count unkeyed starts, duplicate starts and late ends
- Give ok requests the sample end - start and every other outcome and censored request +infinity; count samples as measured requests started, so a lost or refused request still counts; waiting time is one sample per request enqueued, +infinity when removed without a grant
- Treat underpowered statistics by who starts the class: for open-started classes an underpowered run is left out and a null bucket skipped; otherwise both do not satisfy; time-to-recover always needs every bucket of its hold range non-null and satisfying
- Integrate piecewise-constant signals over [warmup, horizon), with the value in effect at warmup and the last value held to the horizon, and the utilisation denominator the integral of the capacity in effect
- Report nearest-rank percentiles, bucketed series on a grid that divides the window exactly, time-to-first and time-to-recover as durations t - after, and series aggregates max, min and mean; claims scoped to a phase or sub-window
- Compute meters over (t - window, t] from the start-of-step configuration, with null for an empty window or a zero denominator and +infinity samples counted in quantile meters
- Compute cost as counted quantities times exact rational prices, per price, in total and per bucket, for the cost aspect (WG-4669) to report
- Mark a run truncated when it ended at the step limit, in error, or stopped with a measured request open

#### Relationships out

- Depends on → Formal semantics (Lean 4): Metrics are defined in Lean as a function of the run (planned, WG-4715).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Simulation engine: Accumulates the measured outputs of a run, in trace and metrics output modes (planned, WG-4715).
- Reads → Metric claims: Takes one statistic per run from the measured outputs (planned).
- Reads → Cost aspect: Prices the counted quantities and reports cost per run, per bucket and per month (planned, WG-4669 on WG-4715).

#### Contracts bound

- Trace, format version 2 (`trace-v2`): provider, via `simulation-engine-calls-performance-metrics`

#### Known unknowns

- Built (WG-4715, WG-4729): resource metrics; request classes with the request lifecycle, response time, outcomes, throughput, legitimate refusals (false positives) and transition firings, over the window and per series bucket, in both engines. Not built: link and cost metrics, waiting time per class, resource signals per bucket, time-to-first and time-to-recover, meters, and truncation by an open measured request (planned: WG-4741). Warm-up is declared, not detected.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Notes: WG-4715, in progress. Built: the resource metrics of format 2 (utilisation as busy and capacity integrals and max held, queue length as integral and max, waiting time per request enqueued in the window with +infinity for a request removed without a grant or still waiting, nearest-rank quantiles at the input's exact decimals), defined over the step sequence in formal/Exegra/Format2.lean (Measured outputs), accumulated step by step in the Rust engine (v2.rs, Measure), output with the trace or alone with output metrics, and checked differentially on generated workload inputs. The request lifecycle, response time, throughput, outcomes, series, time-to metrics, meters and cost are not built.

### Metric claims

Claims over measured outputs, evaluated over many seeded runs with exact binomial and sign tests, reported with their sample count, seeds and confidence as model results (model-holds), never as verified or supported, and validated against recorded load runs.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Declare the claim method metric-bound: scenarios, metric (kind, subject, statistic, outcomes, window, series aggregate), op and bound, approx, or relativeTo another metric with its own window, fraction, confidence, runs, base seed, negatives (on the claim's scenarios or on variants declaring variantOf), control negatives and calibratedBy
- Run the scenario once per seed, run r using the r-th SplitMix64 output of the base seed (mod 2^64), in metrics mode, and take one statistic per run; a truncated run does not satisfy the bound; an underpowered run is left out when only open workloads start the class and counts as not satisfying otherwise; the test uses the thresholds of the N' runs kept
- Decide model-holds, fails or inconclusive by exact rational binomial tails with N fixed in advance (the minimum N is the smallest with beta^N <= alpha, compared exactly); for approx, use the sign-test order-statistic interval of the median of the per-run statistic, which needs at least 6 runs at 95%
- Report model-holds (never supported or verified) with the scope "model only; parameters as stated", runs, seeds, passes, N', fraction, confidence, the spread of the per-run statistic and the underpowered and truncated runs
- Require each negative to fail and each control negative to hold before reporting model-holds; a base scenario that fails makes the claim violated; fixture results test the engine and method and are not evidence about any system
- Check calibrations on hold-out load runs distinct from the fitting runs, with equal workload, capacities and window, a band widened over repeats, at least 6 kept runs and no truncated run, tolerance no wider than the margin computed from the beta-quantile of the per-run statistic, evidence not expired at the recorded asOf date and bound to the current component revisions; report unvalidated otherwise, and extrapolated outside the convex hull (exact rational linear programming) of the tested points with the same fault set
- Vary every assumed and to-be-measured parameter over its declared range, one at a time and then pairwise among the flagged ones, and list first what flips the verdict
- Refuse at validation a run count that could never hold, and a quantile or mean claim whose expected samples from declared open-workload rates are fewer than 30, per bucket for series claims

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Simulation engine: Runs the claim's scenarios once per seed in metrics mode (planned, WG-4715).
- Reads → Performance metrics: Takes one statistic per run from the measured outputs (planned).

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Claim evaluator: Metric-bound claims are evaluated by the metric claims component (planned, WG-4715).

#### Contracts bound

- Run a scenario (`run-scenario`): consumer, via `metric-claims-calls-simulation-engine`

#### Known unknowns

- Built (WG-4715, WG-4729): the metric-bound method is registered and evaluated on format-2 scenarios derived from a specification, reporting model-holds, violated or inconclusive with N, the passes, the fraction and the confidence, with negatives and control negatives. Not built: calibration, sensitivity, the operating envelope and revision binding (asOf is only recorded) (planned: WG-4741).
- The status model-holds is implemented: it is a ClaimResult status of src/core/claims.js and the verdict of src/core/metric-stats.js, and metric-bound claims report it. Its final name and display follow WG-4703.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/metric-stats.js`, `src/core/metric-claims.js`
- Notes: Built: the statistics (src/core/metric-stats.js: exact binomial tails and verdicts, the minimum run count, passes to hold and to fail, runs to decide, the sign-test interval, underpowered statistics, the margin, run seeds and the claim status with negatives; tests/core/metric-stats.test.js) and the metric-bound runner over engine inputs (src/core/metric-claims.js: one metrics-mode run per run seed through runEngineText, on the thread that evaluates claims, which in the application is the main thread). The method is registered and simulationInput derives format 2 for it. Calibration, sensitivity, asOf and revision binding are not built (WG-4741).

### Traffic recorder

Records the events a running Exegra really produces, named by the event ids of this specification (requests and answers of the persistence client with the development server's own events, change notices, engine-worker messages, commits, claim evaluations, simulator controls, preference and camera storage writes), as a bounded stream with logical sequence numbers, so that the stream can be replayed through this specification (component self-model-replay) and shown in the simulator panel's Live mode.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Application (`application`)

#### Responsibilities

- Keep the last 5000 recorded events in memory as {seq, id, payload, at}: seq is the logical sequence number (1, 2, 3, ...) and the only time the replay and the claim evaluator see; at is performance.now(), kept in the application layer for display and stripped before anything reaches the core (src/app/traffic-recorder.js, forReplay); older events are dropped and counted
- Hand out request ids (nextRequestId), the correlation field requestId of the contract events of this specification; the persistence client sends it as x-exegra-request-id and the development server stamps its own events with it
- Receive events from the producers at the code locations the event descriptions name: src/app/main.js (page, engine load, camera), src/app/session.js (project open, list and read, commits, saves and their answers, external changes, claim evaluation, simulator controls, preference writes), src/app/engine-runner.js (worker messages), src/persist/client.js (requests, the server events of each answer, change notices), src/ui/toolbar.js (export, import), src/ui/realisation-panel.js (generate), src/ui/layout.js (layout preference writes)
- Record texts and bundles as their sha-256 (src/core/sha256.js), as this specification represents them, never the text itself
- Record a write to a store that leaves the store's modelled abstraction unchanged (the selection and view of exegra.session, whether the explorer is open in exegra.layout) as nothing: a stutter step of the self-model

#### Relationships out

- Observes → Persistence client: The persistence client records each request (with its requestId) through the observer the session installs (observeTraffic), then the development server's events returned with the answer.
- Observes → Development-server plugin: The development server returns its own events (ws-*, the answer, inferred external edits) in the x-exegra-trace header of each answer and as trace messages of the change stream; the persistence client appends them.
- Observes → Simulation engine: src/app/engine-runner.js records engine-run-posted, worker-run-done, engine-run-returned or engine-run-refused, worker-terminated and worker-load-failed through the record callback the session passes to createEngineRunner, so the engine runner imports nothing of the application.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application session: Records the session's events, installs the persistence client's observer and reads the stream for Live mode.
- Calls → Visual interaction and application entry: src/app/main.js records page-loaded, the engine load, session-init-requested, camera events and step-pulsed.
- Calls → Responsive layout: src/ui/layout.js records panel-toggled and layout-preferences-written.

#### Known unknowns

- The stream is kept in memory only and is lost on reload; there is no durable record under specs/<project>/.history/ (WG-4780).
- The development server tells its own writes from external edits by the hashes it wrote. A change written by the server for another tab is therefore not seen as an external edit by this tab, and the replay reports its change notice as unpredicted (WG-4781).
- Command-line runs (cli/commands.js) record nothing: the stream covers the browser application and the development server only.

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/app/traffic-recorder.js`
- Notes: Built (WG-4777). The producers live in the modules they observe; the server-side events are produced by tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js and returned with each answer.

### Self-model replay

Replays a recorded stream of real events through the behaviors of a scenario of this specification (live-session), one event at a time, and reports every divergence between what the running application did and what the self-model allows.

- Category: Logical module
- Parent: Semantic core (`semantic-core`)

#### Responsibilities

- Execute the behaviors of the scenario with the step semantics of the engine (phase 1 choice reading the configuration before the step, phase 2 effects in scenario order, the expression language of src/core/expr.js, the engine input of src/core/sim-input.js), delivering the recorded events in recorded order with their recorded payloads; logical time is the recorded sequence number
- Keep the events the model emits as predictions instead of queueing them: a recorded event that some transition of the scenario emits must match a pending prediction of the same event (by its correlation field or requestId when both carry it, else the oldest); any interleaving of predictions that respects causality is accepted, because a real run interleaves independent chains (a network answer, a file-watch notice, a claim evaluation) in whatever order they happen
- Report divergences under the claim real-runs-replay-through-self-model: an event that is not an event of the specification (unknown-event), an event the model emits but did not predict here (unpredicted), a predicted event that no behavior consuming it accepts in its current state (not-allowed), a recorded payload value that differs from the predicted one (payload; correlation fields and values the transition gives as number literals, which stand for counts the model does not track, are not compared; nor are arrays and objects), a prediction not matched within 200 recorded events or by the end of a closed stream (missing), and an expression of the model that fails on the recorded payload (error)
- Calibrate instead of diverging when a predicted value reads a variable that no transition has written since the start of the stream: its declared initial value stands for the environment when the stream started (a file hash on disk, a stored preference), so it takes the recorded value; the step lists the calibration
- Return the replay as a trace (steps with fired, ignored, responsible, configuration, the pending predictions and the divergences of each step) that checkTrace, requestLog, the simulator panel and the scene read like a simulated one

#### Relationships out

- Calls → Simulation contract: Reads the behaviors of the scenario from the engine input (simulationInput).
- Calls → Expression language: Evaluates guards, effect values and emitted payloads.

#### Relationships in

- Calls → Application session: Live mode replays the recorded stream through the scenario live-session (createReplay, replayChecks).

#### Known unknowns

- The engine itself is not driven: it runs a scenario to quiescence and delivers simultaneous events in queue order, so the replay reimplements one engine step in JavaScript without faults. Agreement with the engine is by construction from the same engine input and expression language, not checked against the Rust engine.
- A stream that starts mid-session (after the recorder dropped events, past 5000) is replayed from the declared initial configuration; divergences near its start can come from the missing prefix.
- Divergences a longer real session still shows (WG-4782): the camera saved after a view switch with nothing stored is the camera the fit animation produced, not the last camera the model tracked (camera-keep); a restored camera is saved again with its last digit changed by the view's round trip (camera-apply); a commit that changes only comments or formatting re-evaluates no claims, while claims-on-commit predicts an evaluation for every change of the specification text (missing claims-evaluation-requested); a terminated engine worker never finishes its run, while engine-worker-flow still emits worker-run-done for it (missing).

#### Implementation

- Status: manual
- Modules: `src/core/replay.js`

## Contracts

### Apply a transaction

Project.apply: the single entry through which a project's specification or layout changes (UI, source editor, agent command line). Defined by src/core/project.js and src/core/operations.js.

#### Inputs

- operations (Operation | Operation[]): Typed operations: CreateEntity, DeleteEntity, RenameEntity, SetProperty, CreateRelationship, DeleteRelationship, MovePresentationNode, AddClaim, ModifyClaim, AddState, AddTransition, ReplaceSource, ReplaceLayout.
- label (string): Optional history label.
- origin (string): Optional origin of the change (ui by default, cli, undo, redo, external).
- expectRevision / expectHash (number / string): Optional optimistic concurrency guards against the project's revision or semantic hash.

#### Outputs

- result (TransactionResult): ok, revision after the call, semantic hash, affected entity ids, semanticChanged and the diagnostics of the resulting (or rejected) state; on failure also code and message.
- commit event ({type: 'commit', result, entry}): Delivered synchronously to every subscriber after a successful commit; the history entry says whether spec and layout changed.

#### Preconditions

- The operation list is not empty and every operation names a known type
- When expectRevision or expectHash is given, it equals the project's current revision or semantic hash
- A current model exists unless every operation is ReplaceSource or ReplaceLayout
- Each operation's own preconditions hold (the entity exists and has the right kind, new ids match the id pattern and are unused, parameters have the right shape)

#### Postconditions

- Atomicity: when the result is not ok, the specification text, layout text, model, revision, history and undo stacks are exactly as before
- The resulting specification, if the specification text was edited, has no validation errors; a transaction whose result would have errors is rejected
- On success the revision has increased by exactly one, a new immutable model snapshot derived from the new text exists and the undo stack gained one entry (the redo stack is cleared)
- Presentation operations (MovePresentationNode, ReplaceLayout) never change the specification text or the semantic hash
- Existing comments and formatting outside the edited ranges are preserved

#### Errors

- op.empty: The transaction has no operation.
- op.unknown: An operation has an unknown type.
- revision.stale: expectRevision or expectHash does not match the project.
- model.unavailable: The current source has syntax errors and an operation other than ReplaceSource or ReplaceLayout was submitted.
- validation.failed: The resulting specification would have validation errors, or a replacement source has syntax errors.
- layout.invalid: The resulting layout would be unreadable.
- op.param / op.kind: An operation parameter is missing or has the wrong shape, or an entity kind cannot be created.
- entity.unknown / entity.kind: An id names no entity, or an entity of the wrong kind.
- id.format / id.duplicate: A new id breaks the id pattern or is already used.
- delete.system / delete.referenced: The system entity cannot be deleted; an entity that others refer to is deleted without cascade.
- edit.syntax / edit.not-array: A text edit would leave the specification syntactically invalid, or targets something that is not an array.

#### Obligations

- Callers must show the rejection and its diagnostics to the user or agent; a rejected transaction is never retried implicitly.

### Write a project file

Writing spec.jsonc or layout.jsonc of a project: persistence client, POST /__exegra/write of the development-server plugin, and the workspace service's write function in tools/workspace.js.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name matching ^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$.
- file ('spec' | 'layout'): Which project file.
- text (string): Complete new file text, at most 5 MB. In simulated traces the text stands for itself by its hash.
- baseHash (string | null): sha-256 hash of the file text the caller last read or wrote; null means the caller expects the file to be absent.
- meta ({label, origin}): Optional journal annotations.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- success ({ok: true, hash}): The hash of the text now on disk.
- conflict ({ok: false, conflict: true, current: {text, hash}}): The file's current content, returned instead of writing; HTTP status 409.
- failure ({ok: false, error, code}): Any other refusal or failure, with the error code (HTTP 400, 403, 404, 413 or 500).
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Preconditions

- The project name is valid and the target path stays inside the project directory
- The file is spec or layout and the text is a string within the size limit
- Over HTTP: the Host and any Origin are loopback and the x-exegra-client header is present

#### Postconditions

- Optimistic concurrency: the file is replaced only if the sha-256 of its current text equals baseHash (absent equals null); otherwise it is left untouched and the conflict result carries its current content
- Replacement writes a temporary sibling file and renames it over the target; writes of one project are serialized
- After the replace, one line is appended to .history/journal.jsonl; if the append fails the file stays replaced, no line is written and the write is answered as a failure (record write-journal)

#### Errors

- conflict: The file on disk differs from the base hash.
- name.invalid / path.unsafe: The project name is invalid or the path escapes the project.
- file.unknown / input.invalid / too-large: The file is not spec or layout, an input has the wrong type, or the text exceeds 5 MB.
- project.invalid / not-found: project.jsonc is malformed or names a missing file (HTTP 400, thrown by the workspace).
- body.invalid / body.too-large: The body is not a JSON object (400) or is larger than 6 MB (413).
- route.unknown: No such endpoint (404).
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

#### Obligations

- A client that receives a conflict must not overwrite silently: the session keeps the conflict until the user chooses to reload from disk or overwrite disk.

### Project a model into a scene

project(model, viewId, ctx) in src/core/projections.js, followed by layoutScene(scene, pinned, {method, aspect}) or chooseLayout(scene, pinned, {aspect}) in src/core/layout.js.

#### Inputs

- model (Model): The committed model snapshot.
- viewId (string): A declared view, or the id of an implicit view; an unknown id falls back to the first view.
- ctx (ProjectionContext): Collapsed and hidden sets, isolated subsystem, focused claim and scenario, the focused entity (for the references projection), claim results, simulation trace and step.
- layout options ({method?: string, aspect?: number}): The layout method (layered, layered-down, packed, radial or auto) and the target width-to-depth ratio of the view; defaults are layered and 1.6.
- pinned (Record<sceneNodeId, [x, z]>): Absolute positions of leaf nodes from the layout file.

#### Outputs

- scene (SceneModel): Nodes, edges, legend and notes.
- placement (Placement): A rectangle on the ground plane for every node; chooseLayout also returns the method it chose and the score of every method.

#### Postconditions

- Every node and every edge of the scene is backed by an entity of the model (it carries that entity's id)
- Pure and deterministic: the same inputs give the same scene and placement, and the model is not changed
- Position carries no meaning: containment and relationships do

#### Obligations

- The renderer draws only what a scene and a placement contain and reads nothing from the model.

### Evaluate claims

evaluateClaims, evaluateClaim and checkTrace in src/core/claims.js.

#### Inputs

- model (Model): The model whose claims are evaluated.
- recorded (Recorded | null): Recorded execution results of evidence, keyed by evidence id (results), and outcomes an external tool recorded per claim (claims, see record external-claim-outcomes).

#### Outputs

- results (ClaimResult[]): One result per claim in declaration order: status, evaluator, semantic hash, scope, explanation, assumptions, involved entities, counterexample and evidence.

#### Postconditions

- A status is verified only when an evaluator checked the claim on the model; a selector that selects nothing is inconclusive, not verified
- A claim whose method is not registered is not evaluated
- Trace-based statuses state that only the listed scenarios' traces were checked
- Evidence can only make a model-evaluated claim worse (a recorded fail or contradicting evidence turns verified into violated), never better
- An external-evidence claim is supported only by a recorded passing test or by non-test evidence, and says whether Exegra executed anything
- A metric-bound claim is reported model-holds, violated or inconclusive with its run count and confidence, never verified or supported
- Deterministic: no clock, randomness or I/O. Trace and metric claims run the engine synchronously on the caller's thread; in the application that is the main thread, inside every semantic commit

#### Errors

- unknown claim: evaluateClaim is called with an id that is not a claim; it throws.
- evaluator failure: An exception while evaluating a claim becomes an inconclusive result rather than aborting the evaluation.

### Run a scenario

runScenario, createSimulation and createTraceCursor in src/core/engine.js, on the Rust engine compiled to WebAssembly; semantics are specified in docs/module-contracts.md.

#### Inputs

- model (Model): The model holding the behaviors.
- scenarioId (string): A scenario of the model.
- faults (string[]): Optional fault ids replacing the scenario's own list.

#### Outputs

- trace (Trace): Initial configuration, every step with fired transitions, ignored events, faults and the configuration after it, the outcome and the final configuration.

#### Preconditions

- The scenario, every behavior it lists and every active fault exist in the model

#### Postconditions

- Deterministic: the same inputs give deep-equal traces
- The run ends quiescent (queue empty), at the step limit (maxSteps, default 1000) or in error (an expression failed; the failing step carries the error)
- Recorded steps are not changed by later steps

#### Errors

- unknown identifier: The scenario, a listed behavior or an active fault does not exist; a plain Error is thrown.

### Agent command

One invocation of `node cli/exegra.js <command> [args] [--project <name>] [--specs <dir>] [--pretty]`; described in docs/agent-interface.md.

#### Inputs

- command (string): projects, validate, list, show, deps, contracts, claims, evaluate, gaps, ops, apply, plan-delete, simulate, prove, trace, generate, markdown, traceability, verify or help.
- options (flags): Command options and positionals; --project defaults to exegra and --specs to the repository's specs directory. apply reads its operations from --file, --ops or stdin. In simulated traces options carries the one argument a scenario needs.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire: one process answers one invocation; the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775) numbers invocations.

#### Outputs

- envelope ({ok: true, command: string, project: string, semanticHash: string, result}): One JSON document on stdout (cli/exegra.js main, built by runCommand in cli/commands.js); the failure envelope {ok: false, command, project, error: {code, message}, diagnostics} is the error role.
- progress notes ({notes: string[]}): Lines on stderr such as "verify: running <ref>" (ctx.log); not part of the stdout document.
- exit code ({exit: number}): 0 ok, 1 the command failed, 2 usage error (exitCodeOf).
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id assigned by the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775).

#### Postconditions

- Every mutation is applied through Project.apply; no command edits specification text directly
- A rejected or stale transaction, or a disk conflict, leaves the files unchanged apart from the case noted on agent-cli (spec written, layout conflicting)

#### Errors

- usage.unknown-command / usage.invalid-argument / usage.missing-argument: Missing or unknown command or argument (exit code 2).
- revision.stale / write.conflict: The expected hash does not match, or a file changed on disk since it was read; for apply, a layout conflict after the specification was written says so in the message.
- validation.failed / model.unavailable / op.unknown / op.empty / op.param / entity.unknown / entity.kind: The transaction was rejected, or the project has syntax errors, or an argument names no entity of the right kind.
- project.not-found / simulate.failed / verify.failed / generate.unavailable: The project does not exist, the engine refused or could not run the scenario, the specification has syntax errors, or the generators are missing.
- markdown.none / markdown.render / markdown.unknown / markdown.path: The markdown command has nothing to render, fails, is asked for an unknown document or would leave its output directory.
- internal.error: Any other exception.

### Engine input, format version 2

Derived (WG-4664, WG-4715, WG-4729): simulationInput returns {format: "exegra-sim-input", version: 2} for a specification using resources, workloads, links, instances, timers or seeded randomness (needsFormat2 in src/core/sim-input.js), and the Rust engine runs it with its version-2 semantics (engine/exegra-engine/src/lib.rs, v2.rs). Every version-1 field keeps its meaning with times as integers of the base unit. Remaining (WG-4741): crash-instance, meters and the cost metric are not run, and there are no format-2 goldens. Normative definition: docs/simulation.md, "Engine input and trace format version 2".

#### Inputs

- version (2): Version-1 inputs are still run by the version-1 semantics; any other value is refused.
- time ({unit: "tick" | "s" | "ms" | "us" | "ns", perSecond: null | 1 | 1000 | 1000000 | 1000000000}): Base unit; times and durations are integers in [0, 2^53). Unit tick allows no rate or bandwidth.
- scenario.seed (decimal string | null): Unsigned 64-bit seed without sign or leading zeros; required when any element draws; not needed when the only random sites are choice values.
- scenario.stepLimit (integer in [1, 2^53)): maxSteps of the specification.
- scenario.horizon / warmup / stopAt (integer | null): Horizon required with workloads; warmup < horizon <= stopAt.
- scenario.output ("trace" | "metrics"): Full trace, or metrics and final configuration only.
- scenario.steps[].target ({behavior, instance}): Optional addressee of an input step.
- behaviors[].instances ({count: integer} | {key: string, max: integer}): Counted or keyed instances; keyed machine keys keep the canonical JSON of the key, quotes included.
- transitions[].effects (Effect[]): Adds emit with after as integer or distribution, target "reply", via, size and fanout; use, acquire, release with class; timer, cancel; set with {sample: dist}.
- routes ({event, behavior, by: "key" | "round-robin" | "random" | "any" | "least-busy", field?, resource?}[]): Instance routing decided when the entry is created; down instances are skipped except by key; any is enumerated by the explorer.
- resources ({id, kind, capacity, discipline: "fifo" | "priority", queueBound, overflow: "refuse" | "drop" | "shed-oldest", demandFactor: [n, num, den][] | {usl: {alpha: [num, den], beta: [num, den]}} | null, classes: {id, priority, reserved, limit, queueBound, maxWait}[]}[]): Resources with capacity, request classes, queue rules and load-dependent demand (a step table whose last row holds beyond it, or USL parameters evaluated exactly at each grant).
- links ({id, from, to, latency: Dist | null, bandwidth: integer | null, queueBound: integer | null, ordered: boolean, fanout: boolean}[]): Bytes per second, a latency distribution, ordering and per-receiver fan-out queues.
- workloads ({id, kind: "open" | "closed" | "population", ...}[]): Open: event, process, phases, repeat, batch, clients {count, pick, addresses}, legitimate, maxArrivals, payload. Closed: event, population, think, start, reply, timeout, payload, legitimate. Population: behavior, count, start, clients, legitimate. Phases: from < until, sorted, non-overlapping; rates count instants.
- unitExponential ([u, x][]): The pinned unit-mean exponential quantile table at scale 2^-64 on the standard grid (x below 2^72), present when a workload is Poisson.
- requests ({id, start: {event, workload?}, key, ends: {event, outcome: "ok" | "refused" | "timeout" | "error"}[]}[]): Request classes measured by the metrics.
- meters ({id, of, measure: "count" | "rate" | "ratio" | "quantile", window, q?}[]): Windowed measurements readable in expressions.
- prices ({id, counts, price: {num, den}, currency}[]): Exact rational prices per counted unit (a delivery, an ended request, a byte, a resource unit held for one second, an instance up for one second).
- metrics ({quantiles: string[], series?: {bucket}}): Quantiles as exact decimals (default 0.5, 0.9, 0.99) and an optional series bucket dividing horizon - warmup.
- faults[] (Fault): Optional window {at, until} (not on crash-instance); adds scale-demand, set-capacity, set-class-limit, collapse-classes, replace-demand and crash-instance {behavior, instance, at, restartAfter?, notify?}.
- Dist ({kind: "fixed", value} | {kind: "uniform", lo, hi} | {kind: "table", points: [u, x][]} | {kind: "choice", values}): Integer distributions; continuous ones are lowered to tables with the pinned quantile tables and 96-bit fixed-point exp and ln.

#### Outputs

- trace (Trace (version 2)): See contract trace-v2.

#### Preconditions

- The input was derived from a validated specification, or is a generated input of the version-2 generator
- Every integer is within its bound (docs/simulation.md, Integers and bounds; distribution tables below 2^53, unitExponential below 2^72); a specification duration that is not a whole number of base units is a validation error, never rounded
- No reserved name is used, ids of behaviors, resources, meters, links and workloads are distinct, and every reference resolves
- Phases are valid: from < until, sorted, non-overlapping, and repeat at least the last until
- Reserved units sum to at most capacity, each class's reserved units are at most its limit, and every request can be granted, for every fault mask the scenario admits
- A keyed behavior's max is at least the sum of the counts of the populations on it
- A seed is present when any element draws

#### Postconditions

- Two calls of simulationInput on the same model give identical documents
- A specification that uses no format-2 construct still derives a version-1 input, unchanged

#### Errors

- refusal: In order after the version-1 refusals: unknown time unit; Field "<path>" must be an integer in [<lo>, <hi>].; a malformed seed; a reserved name; an unknown id; a malformed table; a Poisson phase whose mean interarrival is below 100 base units; a rate or bandwidth under unit tick; reserved units above capacity or above a class limit, or a request that can never be granted; a window on crash-instance; a population larger than its behavior's max; Input samples "<stream>" but declares no seed.; via together with after; workloads without a horizon, warmup >= horizon or stopAt < horizon.

### Trace, format version 2

Returned by the Rust engine for a version-2 input (engine/exegra-engine/src/v2.rs) and read by the metric-bound claims (src/core/metric-claims.js): the version-1 trace with integer times, instances, resource and link state, draws and metrics. A version-1 input still returns a version-1 trace. Remaining (WG-4741): crash-instance, meters and the cost metric. Normative definition: docs/simulation.md, "Engine input and trace format version 2".

#### Inputs

- input (engine-input-v2): The engine input.

#### Outputs

- version, timeUnit, seed (2, string, decimal string | null): Copied from the input.
- configuration ({time, machines, pending, resources, links, timers, workloads, meters, routeCounters, streams, down, incarnations}): Machines keyed <behavior> for a single undeclared instance, else <behavior>#<index or canonical JSON key>; holdings per instance and class; timers by stable id; draw counters per stream; pending entries carry scheduledAt and, when addressed to an instance, its incarnation.
- steps[].kind ("deliver" | "drop" | "delay" | "service-done" | "wait-timeout" | "link-done" | "fault-edge" | "client-timeout"): Internal entries are steps of their own and count against the step limit.
- steps[].event ({id, payload, seq, source, scheduledAt, target?, from?, routes?, timer?, incarnation?}): Sources add arrival, timer, resource, link and fault-edge kinds.
- steps[].fired[].instance / ignored[].instance (number | string): The instance the transition ran on; ignored reason adds no-instance.
- steps[].resources ({resource, class, request, action, at, waited?, served?, reason?}[]): enqueued, granted, completed, released, refused, shed, dropped, timed-out, in the order they happened.
- steps[].draws ({stream, index, value}[]): Every random draw of the step in order (for an arrival instant: arrival, batch, then client and payload per entry); value as a decimal string.
- steps[].faults[] ({fault, effect}[]): Adds effects scaled, capacity, limit, collapsed, replaced, crashed, stale and restarted.
- outcome, truncated ("quiescent" | "step-limit" | "error" | "stopped", boolean): A truncated run never satisfies a metric claim.
- metrics (Metrics): Integer measured outputs over [warmup, horizon), from the request lifecycle of docs/simulation.md: requests start when their start entry is first taken as head, a lost start ends as error, only ok requests are finite samples, and counts of unkeyed, duplicate and late ends are reported; nearest-rank percentiles and optional series.
- initialDraws ({stream, index, value}[]): Draws made at the start of the run, in order.

#### Preconditions

- The input is a valid version-2 engine input

#### Postconditions

- Deterministic: the same input, seed and faults give deep-equal traces and metrics
- Every number in the trace other than variable and payload values is an integer
- Held units stay within class limits and capacity, and waiting requests within their bounds, at every settled step (a mid-run capacity cut keeps held units until released)
- The lift of a version-1 input that meets the side conditions of lift_agrees gives this trace minus the version-2 fields equal to the version-1 trace
- With output metrics, the metrics equal those computed from the full trace of the same input

#### Errors

- failed step: Version-1 failures, plus: Value <what> exceeds 2^53.; Value demand scale of "<r>" exceeds 2^32.; Reference "<path>" is ambiguous: "<behavior>" has <n> instances.; Aggregate "<path>" needs numbers, got <type>.; Behavior "<b>" would exceed <M> instances.; Route of "<event>" to "<behavior>" needs payload field "<field>".; Instance "<key>" releases <n> units of "<r>" class "<c>" but holds <m>.; Effect replies to "<event>", which has no requester.; Size of emit "<event>" must be an integer in [0, 2^40), got <value>.

### Read a project

GET /__exegra/project?name=: the persistence client's readProject (src/persist/client.js), the development-server route and tools/workspace.js read().

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- files ({spec: {text, hash, path}, layout: {text, hash} | null, recorded: object | null}): The specification with its sha-256 and its path relative to the repository, the layout or null, and the parsed evidence/results.json or null. In simulated traces spec and layout carry the hashes only.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Preconditions

- The project name is valid
- Over HTTP: the Host and any Origin are loopback and the x-exegra-client header is present

#### Postconditions

- Nothing on disk changes
- The hashes are those of the texts returned

#### Errors

- not-found: The project has no specification (HTTP 400).
- name.invalid / project.invalid: Invalid name or malformed project.jsonc (HTTP 400).
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### List projects

GET /__exegra/projects: src/persist/client.js listProjects, tools/workspace.js listProjects (every directory under specs/ with a spec.jsonc or a project.jsonc).

#### Inputs

- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- list ({projects: {name, title}[]}): Project names with the title read from each specification.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Postconditions

- Nothing on disk changes

#### Errors

- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### Subscribe to project changes

GET /__exegra/events?name=: a server-sent-events stream (src/persist/client.js watchProject, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js openEvents, tools/workspace.js watch). The stream answers with no response of its own; each change is a one-way message of contract file-change-notice.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name.

#### Preconditions

- The Host and any Origin are loopback (the x-exegra-client header is not required for the stream, since EventSource cannot send it)

#### Postconditions

- The subscription takes the current hashes of spec, layout and recorded results as its baseline; a change made while no stream is open is never notified
- A ping comment keeps the stream alive every 25 s; the browser reopens a dropped stream

#### Errors

- name.invalid: Invalid project name, before any header is sent.

### File change notice

One server-sent event "change" with data {file, hash}: written by tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js openEvents when tools/workspace.js watch() sees a tracked file's hash change after an 80 ms debounce. Own writes are notified too; the session filters them by hash.

#### Inputs

- file ('spec' | 'layout' | 'recorded'): The file whose hash changed.
- hash (string | null): Its new sha-256, or null when it was removed.

#### Postconditions

- A one-way message: nothing answers it
- Changes under .history/, generated/ and *.tmp are not notified

### Export a project bundle

GET /__exegra/export?name=: src/persist/client.js exportProject, tools/workspace.js exportProject.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- bundle ({format: 'exegra-project', version: 1, name: string, files}): files: {"spec.jsonc", "layout.jsonc"?, "evidence/results.json"?} as read from disk at that moment. In simulated traces files carries the specification's hash.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Postconditions

- Nothing on disk changes
- The bundle holds the files on disk, not the session's in-memory text: an edit whose save has not landed is not in it

#### Errors

- not-found: The project has no specification (HTTP 400).
- name.invalid: Invalid project name.
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### Import a project bundle

POST /__exegra/import {name, bundle}: src/persist/client.js importProject, tools/workspace.js importProject. Outside the transaction system: claim edits-only-via-transactions is violated by this path.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name to create or replace.
- bundle (object): An exegra-project bundle. In simulated traces it carries the specification's hash.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- done ({ok: true}): Every file of the bundle was written.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Preconditions

- The bundle passes validateBundle (format, version, known file names, strings within 5 MB); the toolbar has refused a specification with syntax errors (analyzeSpec), but not one with validation errors

#### Postconditions

- The files present in the bundle are overwritten one after another with atomic renames, without a base-hash check and without a journal line; files absent from the bundle are left alone
- A running session sees the change through its watcher like any external change

#### Errors

- bundle.invalid / too-large: The bundle is malformed: HTTP 400 {ok: false, error} with no code field; the recorder labels it bundle.invalid.
- name.invalid / body.invalid / body.too-large: Invalid name or body.
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### Write generated artifacts

POST /__exegra/generated {name, files}: src/persist/client.js writeGenerated (from the Realisation panel), tools/workspace.js writeGenerated.

#### Inputs

- name (string): Project name.
- files (object): Relative .json or .md paths under generated/ to their text. In simulated traces it carries the semantic hash the documents were derived from.
- requestId (number): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Outputs

- written ({ok: true, written: string[]}): The files whose content changed and were rewritten.
- correlation ({requestId: number}): Correlation id. Not on the wire today: the traffic recorder (planned, WG-4775 layer 2) assigns it per call in src/persist/client.js request() and stamps the response with the id of the request it answers.

#### Postconditions

- Each changed file is replaced atomically under specs/<project>/generated/; unchanged files are skipped
- No base-hash check, no journal line, no change notice (the watcher ignores generated/)

#### Errors

- path.unsafe / input.invalid: A key is not a relative .json or .md path inside generated/, or a value is not a string.
- name.invalid / body.invalid / body.too-large: Invalid name or body.
- host.forbidden / origin.forbidden / client.header: The request is not from a loopback host or origin, or lacks the x-exegra-client header (HTTP 403, tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js checkRequest).
- internal: Any other exception: HTTP 500 {ok: false, error: "Internal error.", code: "internal"}, logged to the Vite logger.

### Run in the engine worker

The messages between src/app/engine-runner.js and src/app/engine-worker.js: one engine input as JSON text in, one trace as JSON text or an error out, paired by id.

#### Inputs

- id (number): The runner's sequence number for the job.
- input (string): JSON text of the SimulationInput (format 1 or 2). In simulated traces the scenario id stands for it.

#### Outputs

- reply ({id: number, ok: true, trace: string}): JSON text of the trace.

#### Postconditions

- A reply carries the id of its job; the runner ignores a reply whose id is no longer waiting
- A new run while one is waiting terminates the worker, so the old job is never answered; the session also discards a run whose token is stale

#### Errors

- engine.refused: The engine refused the input or could not be loaded: {id, ok: false, error}; the recorder labels it engine.refused.

## Behaviors

### Interaction layer: submit an edit and show its outcome

The scene, inspector or a dialog submits a typed operation and later shows the result: accepted, or the rejection with its diagnostics.

- Subject: Visual interaction and application entry
- Initial state: Idle

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| refusalShown | boolean | false |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| interaction-idle | Idle | no |
| interaction-awaiting | Awaiting the transaction result | no |
| interaction-done | Edit accepted | yes |
| interaction-refused | Edit refused and rejection shown | yes |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| interaction-submit | Idle | Awaiting the transaction result | Edit requested | - | emit operation-submitted | no |
| interaction-accept | Awaiting the transaction result | Edit accepted | Transaction committed | - | - | no |
| interaction-refuse | Awaiting the transaction result | Edit refused and rejection shown | Transaction rejected | - | set refusalShown = true | no |

### Source editor: apply a draft and stay in sync

The editor shows the committed text. Applying a draft submits ReplaceSource; a rejection keeps the draft as typed. After any commit the editor shows the committed revision.

- Subject: Source editor
- Initial state: In sync with the model

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| shownRevision | number | 1 |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| source-in-sync | In sync with the model | no |
| source-applying | Applying a draft | no |
| source-draft-kept | Draft kept after a rejection | yes |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| source-apply | In sync with the model | Applying a draft | Source apply requested | - | emit operation-submitted | no |
| source-refresh-after-apply | Applying a draft | In sync with the model | Model published | - | set shownRevision = event.revision | no |
| source-keep-draft | Applying a draft | Draft kept after a rejection | Transaction rejected | - | - | no |
| source-refresh-after-visual-edit | In sync with the model | In sync with the model | Model published | - | set shownRevision = event.revision | no |

### Transaction system: check, validate, commit or reject

Preconditions are checked and text edits computed; the candidate is parsed and validated. With no errors it commits and the revision increments; otherwise it is rejected and nothing changes. Precondition failures, which reject before any text is computed, are abstracted into the rejection.

- Subject: Transaction system
- Initial state: Idle

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| revision | number | 1 |
| rejected | boolean | false |

#### States

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| tx-idle | Idle | no |
| tx-validating | Candidate being parsed and validated | no |

#### Transitions

| Id | From | To | On | Guard | Effects | Fault only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| tx-begin | Idle | Candidate being parsed and validated | Operation submitted | - | emit text-edits-computed | no |
| tx-commit-unvalidated | Candidate being parsed and validated | Idle | Candidate model parsed | - | set revision = revision + 1; set rejected = false; emit transaction-committed with revision = revision | yes |
| tx-reject-partially-applied | Candidate being parsed and validated | Idle | Validation failed | - | set revision = revision + 1; set rejected = true; emit transaction-rejected | yes |
| tx-commit | Candidate being parsed and validated | Idle | Validation passed | - | set revision = revision + 1; set rejected = false; emit transaction-committed with revision = revision | no |
| tx-reject | Candidate being parsed and validated | Idle | Validation failed | - | set rejected = true; emit transaction-rejected | no |

### Session: refresh on commit, then save asynchronously

onCommit re-evaluates claims, re-projects and re-lays-out the scene (the renderer receives it), then announces the model to the inspector and source editor, and starts the flush. The flush writes later and asynchronously; the save state follows its result.

- Subject: Application session
- Initial state: Saved and in sync

| session-on-saved | Save in flight | Saved and in sync | Save succeeded | - | set saveState = 'saved' | no |

### File service: replace the file or report a conflict

The HTTP hop between the persistence client, the plugin and the workspace service is abstracted. persistedRevision is the revision whose text the file on disk holds; conflict records that the last write was refused because the file on disk differed from the base hash.

- Subject: Workspace service
- Initial state: File matches the base hash

#### Variables

| Name | Type | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- |
| persistedRevision | number | 1 |
| conflict | boolean | false |

| Id | Name | Terminal |
| --- | --- | --- |
